### Portal invitation and membership revocation

`BLD-PORTAL-REVOCATION-04` extends `ARCH-17-CLIENT-PORTAL-ACCESS` for BLD-005 and BLD-024. Before this slice, acceptance excluded revoked membership history and could insert a fresh active membership; portal and organization revocation left pending invitations available. The auth owner (`scoped_activation_dispatch`) reserves `backend/src/modules/portal/auth/portal-auth.service.ts`, its existing `portal-auth-enforcement.spec.ts`, `backend/src/common/portal-auth/portal-jwt-auth.guard.ts`, and new adjacent `portal-jwt-membership-state.spec.ts`. The lifecycle owner (`module_grants_resume`) reserves `backend/src/modules/portal/access/portal-access.service.ts`, new private `lib/portal-membership-status.ts`, new `portal-membership-revocation.spec.ts`, existing `portal-access.service.spec.ts` and `portal-access-tenant-isolation.spec.ts` for necessary fixture changes, `backend/src/modules/organization/core/org-membership-access-revocation.ts`, existing `membership-revocation.spec.ts`, and new adjacent `portal-membership-revocation.spec.ts` for focused organization handoff coverage. The independent reviewer is `client_activation_design`; the coordinator owns evidence and runtime verification. Reviewed source is committed at backend `859f12e79`; remaining end-to-end obligations are recorded below and in the activation audit.

Acceptance must resolve one unambiguous same-organization/contact/audience membership including revoked and deleted history, lock it before claiming the invitation, and activate only a live PENDING or ACTIVE membership. Missing, suspended, revoked, deleted, or ambiguous legacy membership identity receives the existing generic rejection; acceptance never inserts a replacement. This is a conservative legacy policy until the separate activation producer explicitly binds an invitation to a membership and epoch. Explicit future regrant and invitation reissue remain required and are not represented as completed here. Recheck exact tenant, contact, audience, token, pending state, and expiry at the conditional claim; return a minted session only after transaction success. Status suspension/revocation uses membership-before-invitation locking, increments the session epoch, and revokes matching pending invitations in the same tenant transaction. REVOKED remains terminal through the generic status command. Organization-linked revocation must also advance epochs and revoke pending invitations in its existing transaction without introducing a cross-module private helper dependency. Portal session reads require a live matching audience and nondeleted membership as well as the current epoch.

Write meaningful failing tests first. Preserve established permission keys, response contracts, audit behavior, and unrelated writers; add no source comments, migrations, dependencies, or live ticket comments. Serialize production/scoped test TypeScript and size gates. Focused tests and independent review precede exact source commits. Target database lock ordering, concurrent redemption/revocation, RLS, browser, invitation production/delivery, and deployment remain open until directly evidenced.

`BLD-PORTAL-REVOCATION-04` additionally reserves `backend/src/common/portal-auth/portal-claims-schema.ts` to the auth owner. `PortalTokenService` already signs `partyContactId`, but the current payload parser drops it. Retain and validate the existing signed claim, and reject a session whose contact identity no longer matches its membership. This does not replace the separate Party-owned deletion/email-change lifecycle handoff. The existing portal reachability tests remain read-only unless an additional fixture change is demonstrated.
